--- Commit Message ---
LU-20290 lustre: register servicenode NIDs with LNet visibility

Use the existing --servicenode target configuration to register
servicenode NID membership with LNet.

server_lnet_servicenode_update() publishes one entry per target,
keyed on the target service name (lsi_svname), whose members are
the local NIDs the mkfs failover.node list names.

When ptlrpc.target_nid_policy=servicenode, Lustre enables the
corresponding LNet discovery visibility policy. Servicenode grouping
then determines the peer partitions advertised to clients, superseding
the topology-derived self/NUMA grouping while the policy is active.

Client connections register the Lustre service they use and the
connection NID with the corresponding LNet peer. Discovery Push
selection uses this association to select the servicenode visibility
partition appropriate for the client and sends that view to a
registered connection NID reachable from the selected partition,
rather than relying on the peer primary NID.

Keep service registrations associated with the client across LNet
peer restructuring. If a peer is removed without an immediate
replacement, retain the registration independently of the peer and
reattach it when LNet can establish continuity through the original
connection NID or the previous peer primary NID. The registration
lifetime remains tied to the Lustre export.

Apply the same servicenode restriction to target NID information
published to the MGS, including READY notifications. Build filtered
notifications using only the final advertised NID set so that the
MGS IR NID table remains consistent with discovery visibility and
cannot be repopulated with NIDs outside the target's configured
servicenode.

Without target_nid_policy=servicenode, servicenode information is
registered but does not change legacy discovery or IR behaviour.

Document the option in a new man page, including the legacy and
servicenode values, where servicenode groups come from, how uncovered
NIDs are advertised, and how the policy applies to IR NID publication.

Add tests verifying that:

- legacy discovery behaviour is preserved when the policy is disabled;
- the servicenode policy partitions discovery views by servicenode;
- servicenode grouping supersedes self and NUMA grouping;
- a local NI not covered by a servicenode remains discoverable;
- the policy is active before the first target mounts;
- a Push does not fall back to the merged full view;
- a target formatted with --failnode contributes no group;
- IR NID updates respect the servicenode NID restriction;
- an unknown policy value stops the module load.

Test-Parameters: trivial
Test-Parameters: trivial testlist=conf-sanity env=ONLY="57c 57d"
Test-Parameters: trivial testlist=conf-sanity env=ONLY="57e 57f"
Test-Parameters: trivial testlist=conf-sanity env=ONLY="57g 57h 57i"
Test-Parameters: trivial testlist=conf-sanity env=ONLY="57j 57k 57ka"

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-05 15:41:37.000000000) ---
Patch Set 8:

(5 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, comments inline. The new subtests cover the registration side well, but nothing exercises withdrawal: no case unmounts a --servicenode target and checks that its group has left the node's discovery views, which is exactly the path the new tgt_del_notifier()/LNetDiscServiceNodeClear() ordering in server_put_super() exists to protect. Adding an LNet net with `lnetctl net add` after a target has mounted would also be worth a case - the setup helper only adds nets before the first mount, so the tgt_nid_notifier() call site is never reached by a test.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-09-13 11:05:10.000000000) ---
Patch Set 39:

(3 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good; the servicenode registration, the Push-side service binding and the IR filtering all trace cleanly, and the refcount handling around peer transfer/detach/reattach holds up. Nothing here requires a re-spin - the inline items are optional and can ride along on the next refresh.

One coverage gap worth considering: the ~200 new lines in lnet/lnet/peer.c that keep a registration alive across LNet peer restructuring (transfer on merge, detach on delete, the lnet_disc_detached_services holding list, reattach by connection NID or previous primary NID, plus the peer reference handed over with each list entry) are the most intricate part of the patch and nothing exercises them. conf-sanity 57c-57ka all drive view/IR behaviour with a stable peer. A subtest that deletes or merges the client peer on the OSS while the client is connected - say 'lnetctl peer del' followed by a reconnect, or a discovery-driven merge - and then checks that the client's Push is still restricted to its target's servicenode would cover the detach/reattach path and the reference counting with it.

Some concerns, see inline comments. The service-constrained Push appears to key on the client's primary NID rather than the NID it connected through, so the multi-rail case raised on patch set 40 still doesn't get a Push.
